`milestoneRowsHtml()` renders milestones in plain array order (not sorted by `dueDate`) specifically so this reorder sync is actually visible — an earlier version sorted milestone rows by `dueDate` for display, which meant reordering the template only changed anything for a freshly-seeded item (every milestone defaults to the same due date at creation, so a date sort's stable tiebreak happened to preserve array order); for any item whose milestones already had distinct due dates — i.e. almost anything actually planned out — the reorder silently had no visible effect at all. Array order is now the single source of truth for milestone row order everywhere; due dates are shown as data on each row, not used to decide row order.

If the diff is non-empty **and** at least one item currently has that `categoryId`, `saveCategory()` doesn't save at all yet — it closes the category modal and opens the confirm modal instead, describing the impact in plain terms ("add N milestones and/or remove M milestones... on K items... This cannot be undone"), with the actual save + `applyCategoryMilestoneSync()` call deferred into the confirm modal's `action`. Cancelling that confirm discards the *entire* category edit, including the name field — same as Cancel in the category modal itself, since by the time the confirm modal is showing, the category modal is already closed and there's no "back" state to return to. If there's no items-facing diff (new category, no name/add/remove changes, or a category nobody uses yet), it saves immediately with no confirm step — the confirm modal only exists to gate the destructive/item-affecting path.

`applyCategoryMilestoneSync(categoryId, addedNames, removedNames)` is the actual sync, run once per affected item: names in `removedNames` are filtered out of `it.milestones` by exact name match — **including a milestone that's already Complete with an actual date recorded**, since the user confirmed exactly this tradeoff up front; names in `addedNames` are appended fresh (`{status:'not-started', dueDate: it.dueDate, actualDate: null}`), skipped if the item already has a milestone with that exact name (so re-adding a name you'd already got manually doesn't duplicate it). It finishes by recomputing `it.status` via `computedStatusFromMilestones()` if any milestones remain — a sync that removes an item's single worst (weakest) milestone can genuinely change its computed status, same as any other milestone-list mutation (see "Item status roll-up").

### Quick-add scope items, the Unassigned bucket, and the Pending category

`unassignedSectionHtml()` renders a `.ws-section` pinned above every real workstream's own section (`renderStatusView()` prepends it: `main.innerHTML = unassignedSectionHtml() + sections + noWorkstreamsHint`) — always shown regardless of the current sidebar filter (`visibleWorkstreams()` only affects the real per-workstream sections below it), since an item sitting there doesn't belong to any workstream yet and shouldn't require switching to "All Workstreams" to see or triage. It holds every item with `workstreamId == null` (`unassignedItemsSorted()`), and below Editor it's omitted entirely once empty — nothing to show and no way to add anything, so there's no point rendering an empty heading. It still shows (read-only, no add affordance) below Editor once there's at least one item in it. This is also the *only* place a brand-new scope item is created — there's no longer a per-workstream "Add item" button (every workstream's own `.ws-section` lost the one it used to have). Its own `.ws-section-header` renders a plain `fa-inbox` icon (an explicit user request) in place of the `.ws-dot` a real workstream's own header shows — there's no workstream color to represent here, so a transparent placeholder dot (the header's original treatment, kept only for grid alignment) was replaced with a real, meaningful icon instead, the same "give this pseudo-section its own icon rather than an empty placeholder" treatment the sidebar's own "All Workstreams" row already got.

**Adding an item is a deliberately minimal, name-only inline capture — no modal, no workstream picker.** The section's own "+ Add item" button (`onclick="openInlineQuickAdd()"`) swaps in a plain text input in its place (`unassignedQuickAddOpen`, a UI-only module-level flag, not persisted) rather than opening any dialog. `saveInlineQuickAddItem()` (Enter, or blurring the input) creates the item with **`workstreamId: null`** — "Unassigned", the same idea as the Pending category below, just for workstream instead of category — straight into the one category flagged `pending: true` (`pendingCategory()`/`isPendingCategory(categoryId)`, next to `categoryById()`), seeded with that category's own milestone template — just `['Scope Item Confirmed']` — via the normal `milestonesForCategory()`. Both the item's real workstream *and* its real category are decided together, later, once the scope is actually confirmed (see below) — nothing about either is asked up front.

`cancelInlineQuickAdd()` (Escape) discards whatever was typed and closes the input back to the button, unconditionally. Because closing the input this way (either function calls `render()`, which tears the focused `<input>` out of the DOM) fires that same input's own `onblur` right back — which is wired to `saveInlineQuickAddItem()` — both functions flip `unassignedQuickAddOpen` to `false` *before* calling `render()`, and `saveInlineQuickAddItem()`'s very first line is `if (!unassignedQuickAddOpen) return;`. So whichever call happens first (Enter, or Escape's `cancelInlineQuickAdd()`) does the real work; the second call the resulting blur triggers sees the flag already `false` and no-ops instead of reviving or double-creating anything. Blurring with nothing typed is the same "quietly close, nothing created" outcome, just without an explicit key press.

**A stale/unknown `workstreamId` (a deleted workstream, a broken merge, a hand-edited import) now normalizes to `null`/Unassigned too**, not an arbitrary real workstream (`normalizeData()`: `if (it.workstreamId != null && !wsIds.has(it.workstreamId)) it.workstreamId = null;`) — a genuinely intentional `null` is left alone, but a broken reference surfaces in the Unassigned bucket for a human to actually re-triage, rather than silently landing wherever `workstreams[0]` happens to be. Dashboard's per-workstream rollups (`scopedItems = items.filter(it => wsIds.has(it.workstreamId))`) already exclude anything with no real, valid workstream, so Unassigned items simply don't participate in workstream-scoped stats until they're assigned one — consistent with them not really belonging to a workstream yet.

**The out-of-the-box, real/selectable categories themselves come from `DEFAULT_CATEGORIES`** (top of the `<script>` block, see "Categories (Admin mode)" above) — an array of `{name, milestones}`, one per category (Development, Artefact, Activity, Dependency), sourced from `LookupTable_Milestones.xlsx`. `seedDefaults()`/`normalizeData()`'s zero-category fallback both seed straight from it (`DEFAULT_CATEGORIES.map((c, i) => ({..., order: i, dependency: !!c.dependency}))`), so adding, removing, or reordering an out-of-the-box category only ever means editing this one constant.

**The Pending category is reserved, not a normal user category**, even though it lives in the same `categories` array and Admin's category list (with a "Reserved" badge — `renderAdmin()`). `seedDefaults()` always creates it (right after every `DEFAULT_CATEGORIES` entry, at `order: DEFAULT_CATEGORIES.length`) and `normalizeData()` adds one back (`categories.push(...)`) on any save that predates it or has somehow lost it — the quick-add flow assumes exactly one `pending: true` category always exists. `deleteCategoryFromModal()` refuses to delete it outright (see above), and `openScopeAssignModal()`'s category `<select>` filters it out of the choices — it's a one-way waypoint, never a destination to assign *to*.

**Marking the item's "Scope Item Confirmed" milestone Complete is the trigger that asks what it really is.** This is checked by `maybeOfferScopeAssign(it, prevStatus)` — `prevStatus !== 'complete' && it.status === 'complete' && isPendingCategory(it.categoryId)` — called from both places a milestone's status can change into that state: `cycleMilestoneStatus()` (the board's inline click-to-cycle badge, the normal path for a quick-added item with just one milestone) and `saveItem()` (covers editing the milestone's status via the full item modal's dropdown instead). Capturing `prevStatus` before the mutation and only firing on the actual not-complete-to-complete transition means Applying the resulting modal doesn't re-trigger it on some later unrelated save while the item is still sitting at Complete — it only fires again if the status genuinely leaves and re-enters Complete.

`openScopeAssignModal(itemId)` populates a Workstream select (preset to the item's current one — nothing is preselected for a still-Unassigned item, since none of the options match `null`) and a Category select (every category except Pending). **It has no dismiss/"Later" option at all — Apply is the only way to close it** (an explicit user request, "do not allow to later in the assigning step"): the modal's own "Later" button is gone, and `scopeAssignModalBg` is deliberately excluded from both the global Escape-key handler and the generic backdrop-click-to-close wiring at the bottom of the script (every other modal in this app has both) — the same "must complete before closing" treatment the role picker's own mandatory first-launch gate already uses (see "The role picker..." below). It still stays reachable afterward through `itemRowHtml()`'s own status badge — not as a "skip for now" affordance any more, but as a page-reload-recovery path (the page could have been closed/refreshed before Apply was ever clicked): whenever `hasRole('editor') && it.status === 'complete' && isPendingCategory(it.categoryId)`, that badge's `onclick` is `openScopeAssignModal(it.id)` instead of the usual `openItemModal(it.id)` (the pencil "Edit" button in `.item-actions` still opens the full modal regardless, unaffected).

**With zero workstreams in the programme, the "no dismiss" design above turned into a genuine dead end — a user-reported bug.** `populateWorkstreamSelect()` renders a `<select>` with no `<option>`s at all when `workstreams` is empty, and since Apply is the *only* way to close this modal, clicking it was the only option — silently writing an invalid, non-null `workstreamId` (the empty-select sentinel, `''`) onto the item, which then vanished from both the Unassigned bucket and every real workstream section until the next `normalizeData()` pass (load/merge/import) reset the orphaned reference back to `null`. `updateScopeAssignWorkstreamState(selectedId)` fixes this by toggling between the real `#scopeAssignWorkstreamField` and a sibling `#scopeAssignNoWorkstreamsField` — a plain "This programme has no workstreams yet — create one to continue" prompt plus a "+ New workstream" button — based on `workstreams.length`, and disabling `#scopeAssignApplyBtn` outright whenever there's nothing valid to select (the select itself stays in the DOM either way, so the disabled attribute is what actually blocks Apply, not just the prompt covering it visually); `applyScopeAssign()` also refuses to run at all in this state as defense-in-depth, the same disabled-control-plus-function-guard pairing used everywhere else in this app.

**The "+ New workstream" button opens the ordinary workstream modal (`openWorkstreamModal(null)`) on top of the still-open scope-assign modal, rather than requiring the item to be dismissed or half-assigned first.** This is the one place in the whole app two `.modal-bg` overlays are ever deliberately open at once — `#wsModalBg` gets a dedicated `z-index: 60` (every other modal shares the base `z-index: 50`) specifically so it visibly stacks *above* `scopeAssignModalBg`'s own backdrop; without it, DOM order alone (`wsModalBg`'s markup sits earlier in the page) would draw it *behind* instead. Escape/backdrop-click on the nested workstream modal close only that one (`closeWorkstreamModal()`, already excluded — like every path that isn't Apply — from ever touching `scopeAssignModalBg`), dropping back to the still-open scope-assign modal underneath with nothing lost. `saveWorkstream()` gained one line for the "return to caller" half: after the usual save/close/render, `if (scopeAssignItemId) updateScopeAssignWorkstreamState(savedWs.id)` re-populates the scope-assign modal's Workstream field — now that a real one exists — preselecting the workstream just created, and re-enables Apply. This check is safe with no extra flag or nesting-depth tracking: `scopeAssignModalBg` covers the full viewport with no dismiss path, so nothing behind it (including the sidebar's own "+ new workstream" button) is reachable while it's open — the *only* way `openWorkstreamModal()` can ever run while `scopeAssignItemId` is still truthy is through this one nested button.

**A still-Pending item's category can never be changed through the full item modal either — only `applyScopeCategory()` (i.e. this same scope-assign flow) is allowed to move an item out of Pending**, an explicit user request ("prevent change category from unassigned scope items... only the assignment process triggered by completed can change it"). `openItemModal()` disables `#itemCategorySelect` whenever the item being edited currently has `isPendingCategory(it.categoryId)` true — layered on top of, not folded into, the existing role-based disable loop, since this is conditional on the item's own data rather than on who's looking at it, and stays locked even at Editor/Admin. `saveItem()` enforces the same rule server-side as defense-in-depth: for an existing item that's still Pending, `categoryId` is forced to stay whatever it already was, regardless of what the (disabled, but not un-tamperable) select reports.

**The Workstream select gets the identical lock, for the identical reason — a later, explicit user request** ("do not allow to assign a workstream to a pending scope item"). A still-Pending item's `workstreamId` is always `null`/Unassigned (see above); allowing this select to assign a real workstream ahead of `applyScopeCategory()` would let someone half-triage an item — a real workstream, but still the placeholder Pending category and its single-milestone checklist — outside the one flow meant to set both together. `openItemModal()` disables `#itemWorkstreamSelect` under the same `stillPending` flag the Category lock already computes (no second condition needed), and `saveItem()` mirrors `categoryId`'s own defense-in-depth exactly: for a still-Pending item, `workstreamId` is forced to stay whatever it already was (`editingItemForSave.workstreamId`, always `null`) rather than reading the (disabled) select's value.

**A still-Pending item's one "Scope Item Confirmed" milestone can also never be marked Not Applicable** — a related, later explicit user request. Marking it N/A would clear its Due/Actual dates and freeze its status badge to a plain "N/A" label (see "Not Applicable milestones" below) while leaving it permanently uncompletable, stranding the item in Pending forever with no way to trigger `maybeOfferScopeAssign()` at all — the same reasoning already applied to blocking that milestone's *removal* (see `removeMilestoneRow()`'s own Pending guard, and `renderMilestonesEditor()`'s inert Remove button, described above). Blocked at both toggle sites, board and modal, each the same way: `milestoneRowsHtml()`'s `naEditable`/`renderMilestonesEditor()`'s `naToggleHtml` both additionally check `!isPendingCategory(it.categoryId)` (rendering an inert `<span>` instead of a clickable button, same convention as everywhere else in this grid), and `toggleMilestoneNotApplicable()`/`toggleEditingMilestoneNotApplicable()` each refuse outright as defense-in-depth, mirroring `removeMilestoneRow()`'s own guard exactly. The inert span's *off*-state icon is additionally faded to `opacity:.35`, matching the Remove button's own disabled trash icon right next to it — a user-reported inconsistency (the N/A icon read as fully "on" even while genuinely disabled). The *on* state (a milestone that actually is Not Applicable, viewed below Editor) stays full-strength colored regardless — that's a real status worth staying legible, not a disabled control.

**Actual completion date and Dependency are hidden outright in the item modal for a still-Pending item too** — a further explicit user request ("remove for unassigned items External Delivery and Actual Completion date"). Neither is meaningful yet: the item hasn't been scope-assigned to a real workstream/category at all, so there's nothing to record an actual finish date against, and whether it's a dependency isn't a call worth making before the item's real scope is even known. `openItemModal()` computes `stillPending` once, up front (shared with the Category-select lock above), and toggles `#itemActualField`/`#itemDependencyField` display — same "hide outright, don't just disable" treatment. `#itemDependencySpocField` is re-asserted hidden immediately *after* `onItemDependencyChange()` runs (which would otherwise reveal it for the edge case of a Pending item that somehow already has `dependency: true` — an import, a hand-edited file) rather than before, since that call happens later in the same function and would otherwise win. Both fields reappear the moment `applyScopeCategory()` moves the item out of Pending.

**The Pending category's one milestone was renamed from "Scope item confirmed" to "Scope Item Confirmed"** (Title Case) — a cosmetic, explicit user request. It's seeded as a plain name string in two places (`seedDefaults()` and `normalizeData()`'s reseed-if-missing fallback), and nothing in the app branches on the literal string itself (it's never used as a lookup key, only displayed) — so the rename was a two-line data change plus updating comments/tooltip text for consistency, not a schema migration. A pre-existing save with the old lowercase name on an already-created Pending item keeps it as-is (the same "orphaned value is harmless, only new ones get the new name" stance this file already takes elsewhere) — there's no backfill pass for it, since it's cosmetic text, not a value anything compares against.

**That same "nothing matches `null`" gap in `populateWorkstreamSelect()` was a genuine, user-reported bug when it showed up in the *full* item modal instead — `openItemModal()`, not scope-assign.** Editing an already-Unassigned item there and saving *anything at all* — a due date, a name tweak, never touching the Workstream field — silently moved it into `workstreams[0]`: with no `<option>` ever matching a `null` `selectedId`, the browser just defaults an ordinary populated `<select>` to its first option regardless of what the item's real `workstreamId` was, and `saveItem()` read that default straight back out as if the user had actively chosen it. Scope-assign's own copy of this gap (previous paragraph) is fine as-is — forcing a real pick *is* that modal's whole job, an Unassigned item is never staying Unassigned coming out of it. The full edit modal has no such excuse: most edits there have nothing to do with workstream assignment at all. Fixed with a third parameter on `populateWorkstreamSelect(selectedId, elId, includeUnassigned)`: `openItemModal()` passes `true` only when editing an item that's *already* `workstreamId == null`, prepending a matching `<option value="">Unassigned</option>` (`selected`) so the select actually reflects the item's real state instead of silently overriding it; a brand-new item (never Unassigned to begin with — quick-add is the only path into that bucket) and scope-assign's own call both still omit it, unchanged. `saveItem()` reads that empty string back as `document.getElementById('itemWorkstreamSelect').value || null` — every real workstream id is always non-empty, so this only ever converts the Unassigned sentinel back to the same `null` every other Unassigned check in the app already compares against.

"Apply" calls `applyScopeCategory(itemId, workstreamId, categoryId)` — a full swap, not a diff/sync like `applyCategoryMilestoneSync()` (that one reconciles an *existing* category's own template changing over time; this is a one-time jump from the placeholder Pending checklist to a real category's). It sets the item's `workstreamId`/`categoryId` (out of `null`/Unassigned, into a real workstream), tombstones the discarded "Scope Item Confirmed" milestone the same way any other removed milestone is (`deletedMilestoneIds`), replaces `it.milestones` wholesale via `milestonesForCategory(categoryId, it.dueDate)`, and recomputes `it.status`/plan dates from that fresh (all pending) checklist.

**A user-reported gap: the item didn't land at the *end* of its new workstream's list the way a brand-new item always does — it kept whatever `order` it happened to have while still sitting in Unassigned, which usually meant `0` (a quick-added item is typically the only thing in Unassigned at the time).** Since `order` is only ever meaningful *within* whichever bucket (`workstreamId`) an item currently sits in, carrying that stale value across into a workstream that already had its own item at `order: 0` made the freshly-assigned item tie with — and, depending on sort stability, often visually jump ahead of — a real, already-placed item, rather than appending after it. `applyScopeCategory()` now recomputes `it.order` the moment `workstreamId` changes, using the exact same "one past every existing item already in the target bucket" formula `saveItem()`'s own brand-new-item branch already uses (`items.filter(i => i.workstreamId === workstreamId).length`, here excluding the item itself since it's already present in `items`) — so a scope-assigned item always lands at the bottom of its real workstream's list, the same place any other newly-added item would.

## L1 Plans

**Overarching, high-level plans that sit above every workstream — an explicit user request** ("I want to be able to define overaching, high-lvel plans ('L1 plans'). I would then [want] the option to link the workstream milestones to the L1 milestones"). Four design decisions were confirmed directly with the user before building this, and shape everything below:
1. **No rollup, originally** — linking a workstream milestone to an L1 milestone started as pure traceability, never computing the L1 milestone's own status/dates. **A later, explicit user request reversed this for status** ("l1 and l2 should be rolled-up" — see "Status now rolls up through the link" below), computed live at read time, never stored back onto the L1 milestone itself. **Due went through a brief rollup attempt of its own and back** — a further request first extended the same reversal to dates ("show and consider actual date of attached scope items and milestones"), then an explicit follow-up reversed the Due half of *that*: "l1 milestones should have its own due date, not rolled up. this is defined (or imported)." Due is a genuinely independent, always-manual-or-imported value, regardless of link state; only Actual ended up roll-up-only, and deliberately with *no* manual path at all ("Actual date should not be able to set, only rolled up") — see "Due briefly rolled up through the link too, then was explicitly reversed" and "Actual is roll-up-only in the other direction" below for the settled shape.
2. **Its own top-level mode**, not a Planning sub-tab — as workstream-independent as Admin/Settings, so it gets the identical treatment: its own topbar button (`#tabL1plans`), outside the Planning/Dashboard/Review `.view-tabs` pill.
3. **A lightweight entity** — just a name plus an ordered list of its own milestones (name/due date/status). No color, no review cycles, no Action/Decision log — none of a workstream's own machinery.
4. **Many-to-one, originally** — a workstream milestone linked to at most one L1 milestone at a time. **A later, explicit user request reversed this to many-to-many** ("in this modal, change the link item to a checkbox to allow linking to multiple l1 milestones") — see "Linking is now many-to-many" below for the full shape.

**Not a new top-level data shape — the same reuse-`items` trick every second item type in this app rides on.** An L1 Plan is a plain `items` row, `itemType: 'l1plan'`, `workstreamId`/`categoryId` always `null`. Unlike an ordinary scope item, **an L1 Plan keeps a real, non-empty, hand-managed `milestones` array** — it's much closer to an ordinary scope item that simply never gets a workstream or category, just with nothing to seed it from (no category/template — see below) and nothing computed about its own creation.

**This is what makes an L1 Plan free to sync, tombstone, and merge, with zero new plumbing.** `buildIndexPayload()`'s `items` filter is `it.workstreamId === null` — an L1 Plan lands in the index's own `items` array automatically, the identical mechanism an Unassigned scope item already rides. `deletedItemIds`/`deletedMilestoneIds` and the existing, completely unmodified `mergeData()`/`mergeMilestonesArray()` cover an L1 Plan and its milestones exactly as they cover any other item and its milestones — both operate generically on any item/milestone regardless of `itemType`. No new top-level array, no new tombstone list, no new merge function exists anywhere for this feature.

**Reusing `itemRowHtml()` for an L1 Plan's own row needed very little special-casing**, since an L1 Plan, having real milestones, already falls through to the *ordinary* "item with milestones" code paths for free: `editable` (plain `hasRole('editor')`) and `milestoneBadge`/`planDatesHtml` (already branch on milestone count) needed zero changes. Three things needed an explicit `isL1Plan` branch: `subRows` (an L1 Plan's own milestone list is bespoke, not the shared `milestoneRowsHtml()` — see below), `tagsHtml` (rendered as a blank `<span class="item-tags"></span>`, since there's nothing here to roll IT/Business/Budget up from and no rollup is computed for this feature anyway), and — a follow-up fix, since a *zero*-milestone L1 Plan doesn't fall through the plain milestone-count branch the way a milestone-bearing one does — `expandable` (see below).

**Editor+ throughout.** `isL1Plan` drives the same "hide Workstream/Category/Milestones" field-visibility decisions the item modal already has, gating on plain `hasRole('editor')` throughout; L1 Plans sit at this app's standard "restructure the plan" tier, the same one workstreams/scope items/milestones/categories already use. Every L1-mutating function (`openL1PlanQuickAdd`/`saveL1PlanQuickAdd`, `openL1MilestoneQuickAdd`/`saveL1MilestoneQuickAdd`, `cycleL1MilestoneStatus`, `updateL1MilestoneDateField`, `removeL1Milestone`, `openL1ConnectModal`/`toggleL1MilestoneLink`) guards itself with a plain `if (!requireRole('editor')) return;`.

**Milestones are always hand-added, one at a time — there's no category/template to seed an L1 Plan's checklist from at all**, a genuine, deliberate difference from an ordinary scope item's milestones (seeded from a category's template — see "Milestone checklist" above). `itemRowHtml()`'s `l1AddMilestoneHtml` renders an inline "+ Add Milestone" affordance (Editor+) right after an L1 Plan's own expanded milestone list — a name-only inline-capture pattern (Enter or blur to save, Escape to cancel, `l1MilestoneQuickAddOpenFor` tracking which plan's own quick-add is currently open). `saveL1MilestoneQuickAdd()` pushes a plain `{id, name, dueDate: null, actualDate: null, status: 'not-started', notApplicable: false, updatedAt}` — no template, no seeded date, exactly the shape a real milestone always has, just never batch-seeded the way a category-backed item's checklist is. There is no "+ Add milestone" equivalent for an *ordinary* item once it's saved (see "Milestone checklist" above, `addMilestoneRow()` was removed outright) — L1 Plans are the one place in this app where hand-adding a milestone at any time, indefinitely, is the intended, permanent way milestones get created, not a removed legacy affordance.

**The link lives on the *workstream* milestone, not the L1 milestone.** `m.l1MilestoneIds: String[]` is a field on every *ordinary* (non-L1-Plan) item's milestone shape, holding any number of an L1 milestone's own `id`s. There is deliberately no reverse-reference field on the L1 milestone side — "which workstream milestones link to me" is always computed live via `linkedWorkstreamMilestones(l1MilestoneId)` (`items.forEach` over every non-`l1plan` item's milestones, filtered by `(m.l1MilestoneIds || []).includes(l1MilestoneId)`), the same "derive, don't duplicate" instinct several other computed values in this app already follow — there's nothing to keep in sync if a link is added/removed anywhere, since nothing is cached. `findL1Milestone(l1MilestoneId)` is the reverse lookup (an L1 milestone's own id → `{plan, milestone}` or `null`), used both by `normalizeData()`'s orphan-reset (below) and by the read-only indicator.

**Linking is now many-to-many — a later, explicit user request ("change the link item to a checkbox to allow linking to multiple l1 milestones") reversing this feature's original many-to-one design, where `m.l1MilestoneId` was a single nullable string.** `m.l1MilestoneIds` is now always a real array (possibly empty) — `normalizeData()` migrates a pre-existing save's legacy scalar `l1MilestoneId` into the equivalent one-element (or empty) array the first time it loads under this version, then drops the old field outright (`delete m.l1MilestoneId`); nothing reads it again after that pass. Every read site that used to compare `m.l1MilestoneId === id` now checks `(m.l1MilestoneIds || []).includes(id)` instead (the `|| []` matters: a milestone created within the *current* session, before its first `normalizeData()` pass, has no guarantee the field is already present — see the milestone-creation-site list below), and every write site that used to assign the whole field now adds/removes just one id from the array, leaving whatever else is already there untouched. `linkedWorkstreamMilestones()`'s own predicate above is the read-side change; `toggleL1MilestoneLink()`/`toggleL1PickMilestoneLink()` (see "The Unlinked tab and L1 milestone picker" below) are the two write sites. **Every place in this file that constructs a milestone object needed `l1MilestoneIds: []` added explicitly** — the same "don't rely solely on normalizeData()'s own backfill" discipline `notApplicable`/`reportingLevel` already established for themselves — covering `milestonesForCategory()` (an ordinary item's own template seed), `applyCategoryMilestoneSync()`'s newly-appended-milestone path, and `makeL1Milestone()` (an L1 Plan's own milestone factory, still always forced empty — an L1 milestone never links to another L1 milestone, unchanged from the original design).

**A real, user-facing bug was found and fixed while building this: saving an item through the item modal used to silently drop every one of its milestones' links, regardless of what was actually edited.** `openItemModal()`'s own `editingMilestones` copy (the modal's detached working array) explicitly whitelisted which fields to carry over from the real milestone — `id`/`name`/`dueDate`/`status`/`actualDate`/`notApplicable` — and never included the link field at all; `saveItem()`'s own milestone-mapping (`editingMilestones.map(m => ({...}))`) constructed a brand-new object literal with the identical, link-less field list, then wrote that straight over the item's real `milestones` array. So the link was already gone the moment the modal opened, before a user could even do anything about it — renaming the item, fixing a due date, anything at all that reached Save, wiped every link. This predates the many-to-many reversal above (the original scalar `l1MilestoneId` had exactly the same gap) — it just became impossible to ship the new checkbox-based multi-link feature without fixing it first, since the new field would have been just as silently discarded. Fixed by adding `l1MilestoneIds: (m.l1MilestoneIds || []).slice()` to `openItemModal()`'s own copy and `l1MilestoneIds: m.l1MilestoneIds || []` to `saveItem()`'s milestone-mapping — carried straight through unedited either way, since this modal has no UI of its own for the field at all (linking only ever happens via `l1ConnectModalBg`, which writes straight to `items` independently of any open item modal). Verified live: linked a milestone, opened its parent item's modal, renamed the item, saved, confirmed the link (and `linkedWorkstreamMilestones()`'s own count for the L1 side) survived. `APP_VERSION` was bumped for this change — a real bug fix plus a changed data field, per that constant's own "worth other devices knowing about" bar.

**Linking UI (`l1ConnectModalBg`)** — candidates are individual milestones, not whole scope items — **plus a search box**, given the much larger candidate volume (every milestone across every item, not just every item). `openL1ConnectModal(planId, milestoneId)` sets `l1ConnectPlanId`/`l1ConnectMilestoneId` and opens the modal, widened to `760px` (an inline override past `.modal-wide`'s own `600px`, the same precedent the Minutes modal already set) to give a real 5-column table room to breathe. **Every workstream milestone is a real candidate, regardless of what it's already linked to** — this used to exclude a milestone already linked to a *different* L1 milestone outright (on the reasoning that connecting it here would silently steal it from wherever it already belonged), removed once linking became many-to-many (see "Linking is now many-to-many" above) — that concern doesn't apply once a milestone can legitimately belong to several L1 milestones at once; a milestone linked to *this* L1 milestone shows up pre-checked (`(milestone.l1MilestoneIds || []).includes(milestoneId)`), same as before. An L1 Plan's own milestones never appear as candidates (`items.forEach` skips `itemType === 'l1plan'` outright) — an L1 milestone never links to another L1 milestone. `toggleL1MilestoneLink(itemId, milestoneId, checked)` writes straight to `items` the moment a checkbox changes (no detached working copy, no explicit Save) — adds/removes just `l1ConnectMilestoneId` from the milestone's own `l1MilestoneIds` array, leaving any other ids already there untouched; every toggle is trivially reversible by unchecking again.

**The candidate list is a flat, real 5-column table — Workstream / Scope Item / Milestone / Due / Checkbox — an explicit user request replacing the original layout** (one bold heading per workstream, a smaller sub-heading per scope item, then a plain name-only row per milestone with no Due shown at all — leftover naming, `.journey-connect-*`, from the earlier, since-removed Journeys feature this modal's styling was originally borrowed from). `l1ConnectHeaderHtml()`/`l1ConnectRowHtml(item, milestone, checked)` render one real header row and one row per candidate — Workstream and Scope Item are now per-row column values (`.l1-connect-source`, ellipsis-truncated), not group headers, and Due reuses the same `fmtDate()`/em-dash-when-unset treatment `l1LinkedRowHtml()` already established one page over. `renderL1ConnectList()` still sorts candidates so related ones read together even without the old grouping's visual headers: by workstream's own display `order` (`Map` built from `workstreams` sorted by `order`, `Unassigned` always sorting last via `Infinity`, mirroring every other "Unassigned sorts last" convention in this app), then by scope item name within each. Each row is one `<label class="l1-connect-row">` wrapping its own trailing checkbox — clicking anywhere on the row toggles it, not just the small checkbox itself, the same affordance the original layout's own `<label>`-wrapped rows already had.

**Scope Item was then widened twice more in quick succession — 150px → 375px (2.5x, "increase the width of this modal. scope item needs 2,5x the size"), then 375px → 450px ("increase scope item width to 450px")** — with the modal's own fixed width bumped to match each time (760px → 960px → 1035px), always by the identical delta the column itself grew by, so Milestone's own flexible track keeps the same genuinely comfortable `~231px` of room throughout rather than getting squeezed back down toward its bare `160px` floor each time Scope Item grows. `l1LinkedRowHtml()`'s own, unrelated Workstream/Scope Item columns one page over were *not* touched by any of this — every one of these requests was specific to the connect modal's own candidate list, not every place this app shows a Workstream/Scope Item pair.

**Once real Workstream names started truncating too ("Platform & Product Setup"), Workstream widened 130px → 195px (1.5x, "increase workstream size to 150%") — and, in the same request, Scope Item was reduced by that identical +65px ("reduze scope item size"), 450px → 385px.** By then Scope Item's own 450px had turned out to be more room than it strictly needed once repeated-row collapsing + wrapping (see the paragraphs just below) meant it was no longer racing to fit a whole name on one line — so trading that slack back to Workstream, rather than widening the modal yet again, kept both the modal's own overall width and Milestone's own `~231px` exactly where they already were; only the split between the two fixed columns moved (verified live via `getBoundingClientRect()` at the final `1035px` modal width: `195/385/231/90/25`px across the five columns — Milestone's own room provably unchanged from the previous `130/450` split).

**A workstream filter dropdown sits alongside the search box — a later, explicit user request ("provide a dropdown filter to select the workstream")** — composing with it as a plain AND, the same "narrows the list, never overrides the other filter" shape every other search-plus-chip/dropdown combo in this app already uses. `populateL1ConnectWorkstreamFilter()` rebuilds `#l1ConnectWorkstreamFilter`'s own `<option>`s fresh on every `openL1ConnectModal()` call — a workstream added/renamed/deleted since the modal was last open must never show a stale list — with `"All Workstreams"` (an empty value, matching every other "no filter selected" convention in this app) leading and `"Unassigned"` trailing, the identical `Infinity`-sorts-last convention `renderL1ConnectList()`'s own candidate sort already established one paragraph up. `openL1ConnectModal()` also explicitly resets the select's own value back to `''` right after that rebuild, rather than relying on a freshly rebuilt `<select>` naturally landing there on its own (true in a real browser, since no option here ever carries `selected` — but explicit is safer, and is what the JXA test harness's own simplified `<select>` mock actually needs, since it doesn't re-derive `.value` from a rebuilt `innerHTML` the way a real one does — see "Tests" above for that documented harness limitation). `renderL1ConnectList()`'s own filter check reads the select's live value directly (no separate module state, matching the search box's own `l1ConnectSearchInput` pattern) — `'unassigned'` matches only `it.workstreamId == null`, any other non-empty value matches that exact workstream id, and an empty value (the default) matches everything.

**The search box itself was later made "smarter" — a later, explicit user request ("use multiple words. one could match from scope item, one could match in milestone").** The original search was a single substring match against either field (`m.name.toLowerCase().includes(query) || it.name.toLowerCase().includes(query))`) — a genuine multi-word query like `"cutover finance"` never matched anything under that shape, since neither the milestone name alone ("Cutover Complete") nor the item name alone ("Finance Migration") ever contains the whole two-word phrase together. The query is now split on whitespace into individual words; every word must match *somewhere* (an AND across words — a query that doesn't fully match still narrows to nothing, same as a plain single-word search always did), but each word independently checks the item name **or** the milestone name (an OR across fields per word) — so `"cutover finance"` now finds a `"Cutover Complete"` milestone belonging to a `"Finance Migration"` item, correctly excluding another item (`"HR Migration"`) that happens to share the exact same milestone name but not the word `"finance"` anywhere. A single-word query — still the common case — behaves identically to before, since one word trivially satisfies "every word matches". Extra/repeated whitespace between words is collapsed via `.split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean)`, so `"  cutover   finance  "` searches the same as `"cutover finance"`. This is scoped to `renderL1ConnectList()` alone, not Planning's own search box or any other search elsewhere in this app — the request was specific to this one modal's candidate list.

**Two more problems surfaced once this table was actually loaded with real programme data — a user-initiated design review ("is this ui design good. do not code yet"), diagnosed and fixed on explicit follow-up ("do it") rather than another reflexive width bump.** (1) A scope item with several milestones repeated its own, often long, Workstream/Scope Item text identically on every one of those rows — genuinely hard to scan, since the eye had to re-read the same long string on each row just to confirm "still the same item" before it could focus on what actually differed (the milestone name). (2) A genuinely long real name (`"Requirements Package: Blocks / Restrictions - Drop 2 - Event & Transaction Monitoring"`) still truncated even at the already-widened `450px` column — no fixed width was ever going to keep pace with how long a real programme's own names can get, and widening further would have come at Milestone's own expense with no real ceiling in sight.

**Fixed by collapsing the repeated text, not by widening again.** `renderL1ConnectList()` computes, for each candidate by its position among the (already item-grouped, via a stable sort — see the comment on this exact mechanism just above) `candidates` array, `showSource` (is this the first row of a run sharing the same item?) and `isLastInGroup` (is this the last?), and passes both into `l1ConnectRowHtml(item, milestone, checked, showSource, isLastInGroup)`. `showSource` false still renders two real, empty `.l1-connect-source` spans — the usual "never omit a grid cell, or every later column in that one row silently shifts" discipline this app's other grids already follow — rather than omitting them outright. `isLastInGroup` false adds `.l1-connect-row-grouped` (drops that row's own `border-bottom`), so a multi-milestone item's own rows read as one visually connected block, with the border reappearing only once the next item's own group genuinely starts — verified live via CDP with a real two-group scenario (a 4-milestone item followed by a 1-milestone item): the border correctly stayed absent between the four rows of the first group and correctly reappeared between the last row of that group and the first row of the second.

**`.l1-connect-source` itself switched from `overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis; white-space:nowrap` to a plain `overflow-wrap: break-word`** — once a name is only ever rendered once per group instead of four times, letting it wrap onto two (or more) lines is no longer the visual cost it would have been under the old always-repeat shape, and a genuinely long real name is now fully readable in the row itself rather than hidden behind an ellipsis with only the `title` tooltip as a hover-only escape hatch (that tooltip is still set unconditionally on every row, `showSource` or not, so a blank continuation row still orients the reader on hover). The `.l1-connect-row:hover` background/`.l1-connect-row:last-child`'s own border removal are both untouched by any of this.

**Status now rolls up through the link — a later, explicit user reversal of this feature's original "pure traceability, no rollup" decision** ("l1 and l2 should be rolled-up"). `toggleL1MilestoneLink()` still writes only to the *workstream* milestone's own `l1MilestoneIds`/`updatedAt` — linking itself never touches a milestone's own `dueDate`/`actualDate`/`status` in either direction, on either side of the link; "rollup" here is always a read-time *display* computation over already-existing data, never a write. `computedL1MilestoneStatus(l1MilestoneId)` rolls an L1 milestone's own displayed status up from its linked workstream milestones — the same weakest-link `STATUS_SEVERITY` reduce every other computed status in this app uses (via the existing `computedStatusFromMilestones()`), just over `linkedWorkstreamMilestones(l1MilestoneId)`'s own already-resolved status values — returning `null` when nothing's linked, so callers fall back to the milestone's own manual, hand-set status instead. `computeL1PlanStatus(planId)` is one level further removed: it reduces over the L1 Plan's own milestones' *effective* status (each one's `computedL1MilestoneStatus()` when linked, its own manual status otherwise) — never a milestone's raw stored status directly, which would be stale for a linked one.

**The L1 Plan's own "X/Y milestones" badge (`itemRowHtml()`'s shared `done` count, see "Status board" above) missed this same "effective, not raw" distinction until a later, user-reported bug fix — it never incremented for a linked L1 milestone marked Complete purely through the rollup, since it read `m.status` directly** (the milestone's raw field, which the rollup deliberately never writes back onto — see `computedL1MilestoneStatus()`'s own "computed fresh at read time, never stored back" comment just above). `effectiveMilestoneStatus(m)` — `isL1Plan ? (computedL1MilestoneStatus(m.id) || m.status) : m.status` — is the fix, the identical fallback every other L1 milestone display already uses; an ordinary item's milestone has no rollup concept at all, so this is a no-op there. `isL1Plan` itself had to move earlier in `itemRowHtml()` to be available at the point `done` is computed, ahead of where it originally sat.

**Due briefly rolled up through the link too, then was explicitly reversed back to always-manual/imported — the settled shape.** A request extended the status reversal to dates ("show and consider actual date of attached scope items and milestones"), giving Due the same computed-when-linked treatment status has; a direct follow-up reversed exactly that half: **"l1 milestones should have its own due date, not rolled up. this is defined (or imported)."** Due is now, and stays, `m.dueDate` — a genuinely independent value, hand-typed into `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s ordinary `dateInputHtml()` pill or set by `importL1PlansFromExcel()`'s "Target Date" column, completely unaffected by link state; `updateL1MilestoneDateField()` has no link-based guard at all (unlike `cycleL1MilestoneStatus()`'s own guard for status). This independence is load-bearing, not incidental: it's what makes `m.dueDate` a meaningful, non-vacuous comparison basis for the two delayed-color checks below — a value derived *from* the exact linked milestones being checked against it could never fail either check.

**A user-reported regression — "inline editing of the date field is broken again on 4 digit year. after 2 digits, it leaves field" — turned out to be this one pill alone never having received the app-wide `onchange`-removal fix documented under "A third, separate bug"/"armPickerCommit()" above.** `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s `dueHtml` had carried a hardcoded `onchange="if(this.value) updateL1MilestoneDateField(...)"` alongside its `onblur` since this field was first built — every other date input in the app (item Start/Due, milestone Due/Actual, Action Log Due) already had `onchange` dropped entirely in favor of `onblur`-only commits plus `armPickerCommit()` for a genuine native-picker selection, but this one spot was missed. The symptom matches that history precisely: Chrome fires `change` on a date input the instant any single segment looks syntactically complete, and the year segment does that after just its first typed digit — destroying the still-focused input and swallowing every keystroke after. Fixed by removing the `onchange` clause outright; the field's own `pickerArmFn` (4th argument to `dateInputHtml()`) was already correctly wired, so nothing else needed to change. Verified with real CDP-dispatched keystrokes into the year segment of a live input (not synthetic JS event dispatch, which doesn't reliably drive segmented date-input behavior): typing all 4 year digits in sequence left the input focused throughout, and blurring committed the correct final value.

**Actual is roll-up-only in the other direction — the third, later explicit user request that landed alongside Due's reversal** ("also show the actual date... on L1 milestone level. Actual date should not be able to set, only rolled up"). `computedL1MilestoneActualDate(l1MilestoneId)` mirrors `computedL1MilestoneStatus()`'s own shape — same `linkedWorkstreamMilestones()` source, same `notApplicable` exclusion `computedStatusFromMilestones()` already applies (reused directly, not duplicated), same `null`-when-nothing's-linked convention — and **deliberately progressive**: the latest date among whichever linked milestones have one *so far*, not gated on the whole group completing first (confirmed directly, over the alternative of staying blank until every linked piece is done) — it reads as "the latest known date across the group so far," updating as pieces finish or their own dates change. There is no manual path for it at all, linked or not — mirroring `reportingLevel`'s own "computed/imported only, no UI to hand-set it" treatment above — so `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s `actualHtml` is always either this computed value or a genuinely blank cell, never an editable pill the way Due always is.

**Originally reduced over `actualDate` alone — reversed on a later, explicit user request to reduce over each linked milestone's own Due *or* Actual, whichever is later** ("l1 milestone actual date should roll up from linked milestones and reflect latest date from due date or actual date"). `computedL1MilestoneActualDate()` now does `linked.flatMap(m => [m.dueDate, m.actualDate]).filter(Boolean)` before taking the max — mathematically the same as taking each milestone's own later-of-the-two first, then the max across milestones, just flattened into one pass. This means a still-open linked milestone (no `actualDate` yet) now contributes its own `dueDate` to the rollup — so the L1 milestone's own "Actual" cell can show a still-*future*, merely-planned date, not only one that's already happened; once that milestone's real `actualDate` lands, it wins over its own `dueDate` the moment it's later. `isL1MilestoneActualLate()`'s own delayed-color check (comparing this rollup against the L1 milestone's own manual Due) and `l1DelayedEntries()`'s own "L1's own rolled-up Actual is later than its own Due" entry both inherited this broadening for free, with no code changes of their own needed — an attached milestone whose own Due alone (never mind Actual) is already past its L1 milestone's Due now correctly produces *both* signals (the attached milestone's own entry, and the L1 milestone's own), not just the attached one, the identical "two real, independently-true facts, not a duplicate" shape this file's own Delayed-list documentation already established for the Actual-vs-Due case.

**The L1 milestone's own rolled-up Actual is flagged when it lands after its own manual Due — the same `pill-overdue`/`actualLate` convention "Delayed-milestone color coding" above already established for the ordinary status board, extended up to this level by a fourth, later explicit request** ("if the actual date is post the due date, use delayed color"), via the named `isL1MilestoneActualLate(m, computedActual)` predicate. **Originally rendered as plain `style="color:var(--stat-red)"` text on the same `item-dates-computed` span used when on time — reversed on a further, later explicit request to render as a real pill instead** ("if rolled up actual date is post due date of l1 milestones show actual date as red pill (overdue)"): `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s `actualHtml` now swaps the span's whole class to `date-pill pill-overdue` (the exact same class pair `dateInputHtml()`'s own editable Due/Actual pills use when overdue elsewhere in this app) rather than adding an inline color on top of the plain-text span, with the on-time case still the plain `item-dates-computed` text it always was. `.date-pill.pill-overdue` was built for an editable field with a calendar icon inside it, but its background/color/rounded shape read correctly on this plain, non-interactive span too — reused directly rather than defining a near-duplicate class, even though there's no icon here for its own `.date-pill.pill-overdue i` rule to ever match.

**That on-time-vs-overdue split introduced a real, user-reported alignment bug: `.date-pill` (`padding: 3px 8px`, a rounded background) and `.item-dates-computed` (no padding at all) are two entirely different box models, so across several L1 milestones the rolled-up Actual text itself started at a different x-position row to row depending on which of the two states each one happened to be in — a milestone whose Actual was on time read as visually offset from one just below it whose Actual was overdue, even though both rows' own grid columns were correctly aligned underneath.** Fixed by always rendering `.date-pill` as the base class regardless of state, layering `.pill-overdue` on top only for the red tint — `date-pill${l1ActualLate ? ' pill-overdue' : ''}` — the same base-class-plus-color-modifier pattern every other overdue pill in this app already uses (Due/Actual on the ordinary status board are always a real pill too, tinted red only when overdue); this was the one read-only span that had briefly split into two different box models instead of one. An on-time Actual now renders as a plain, neutral-toned pill rather than bare text — a small visual change, but one that also brings it in line with every other pill in this app, not just a narrower alignment patch.

**The whole row is also tinted — originally by the milestone's own effective status, then reversed to a pure date-vs-Due fact check.** First shipped as a later, explicit user request ("highlight the L1 milestone row according to status"): `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()` already computes `effectiveStatus` (`computedL1MilestoneStatus(m.id) || m.status` — the rolled-up value once linked, the milestone's own manual value otherwise) to color the row's own status badge, and the row's `<div class="l1-milestone-row">` carried the identical `style="background:var(--stat-${effectiveStatus}-bg)"`, reusing that exact same value and the exact same `--stat-*-bg` token every status badge in this app already reads from — the same "row tinted by its own status" precedent `.review-confirmed` already established for a confirmed Review row.

**A further, later explicit user request replaced that status-driven tint with a pure fact-based one, independent of RAG status entirely** ("change how the color of the l1 milestone row is determined. Color should be determined by fact, if the due date or actual date of all linked scope item milestones is matching with the target date of the l1 milestones. If all dates are before or on the date, set to green. if its within 1 month after the date, set to amber, if its longer, set to red"). `computedL1MilestoneDelayColor(l1MilestoneId, l1DueDate)` (next to `computedL1MilestoneActualDate()`) is a direct, two-value comparison — the L1 milestone's own manual `dueDate` (never a rollup, see above) against its own rolled-up Actual: on or before is `'green'`, up to one calendar month later is `'amber'` (via a small new `addOneMonthIso(iso)` helper — plain `Date` month-index arithmetic, the same "compose from parts, let `Date` normalize a year rollover" idiom `isoFromDateParts()` next to it already uses), anything later than that is `'red'`. Returns `null` — "nothing to compute a color from" — when there's no manual Due to compare against yet, or the rolled-up Actual is itself `null` (nothing linked yet, or nothing linked has contributed a date).

**Calls `computedL1MilestoneActualDate(l1MilestoneId)` directly for that rolled-up Actual, rather than re-deriving its own copy of the same "latest Due-or-Actual across linked milestones" reduce — a deliberate tightening, prompted by a user asking to confirm the row color was genuinely comparing Due against the rolled-up Actual (the two values were in fact already mathematically identical, since the row's first cut duplicated the exact same `flatMap`/max logic inline; this refactor makes that a hard guarantee instead of two independently-maintained copies that happened to agree today.** No behavior change — the full test suite (including every existing `computedL1MilestoneDelayColor` test) passes unchanged before and after.

**A Complete milestone is a hard override on top of all of this — an explicit user request** ("if a l1 milestone is completed do not apply red, amber, green color coding. use blue"). `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s `rowColor` checks `effectiveStatus === 'complete'` first, before `computedL1MilestoneDelayColor()` ever gets a say: `effectiveStatus === 'complete' ? 'complete' : (computedL1MilestoneDelayColor(...) || effectiveStatus)`. `--stat-complete-bg` is the same blue token the badge, the status board, and every other Completed surface in this app already reads from — reused directly, not a new color. This fires regardless of *how* it went Complete (the milestone's own manual status, or the rolled-up `computedL1MilestoneStatus()` once every linked milestone is itself Complete/weakest-link Complete) and regardless of how badly the underlying dates would otherwise have scored — a milestone that finished 6 months late still shows blue, not red, once it's genuinely done; there's nothing left to flag as "at risk" about something that already happened. The Actual column's own `pill-overdue` treatment (`isL1MilestoneActualLate()`) is untouched by this and still fires independently — a late-but-Complete milestone still honestly shows "Finished after Due" on its Actual cell, just no longer bleeds that same red into the row's own background.

**A user-reported "why is this green, not red" turned out not to be a color-logic bug at all, but a display ambiguity: `l1LinkedRowHtml()`'s Due/Actual columns and `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s own rolled-up Actual all rendered through the plain `fmtDate()` every other read-only date span in this app defaults to — which never shows the year.** An L1 milestone's own linked milestones can legitimately span several years (a target set far out, work due long before it) — a bare "19. Jan." gives no way to tell whether that date landed months before or after whatever it was colored against, which is exactly what made a correctly-computed green row look wrong at a glance in the reported case (the milestone's own rolled-up Actual really was in an earlier year than its Due, so green was correct — the confusion was purely that the year wasn't visible anywhere to confirm that). Fixed by switching all three read-only spots to `fmtDateY()` (always shows the year) instead — the identical "Plan-date ranges always show the year" convention a Start→Due range already established elsewhere in this app, just extended to these two columns. The L1 milestone's own Due cell itself (`dueHtml`, via `dateInputHtml()`) was never affected — it's a real `<input type="date">`, which already renders the OS/browser locale's own full date including year, unlike a plain formatted-text span. Deliberately scoped to just these two functions — the Connect modal's own candidate list (`l1ConnectRowHtml()`) and the Unlinked tab (`renderL1UnlinkedItemsHtml()`) still use plain `fmtDate()`, since neither was part of what was actually reported or asked for here.

`l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s row background is now `computedL1MilestoneDelayColor(m.id, m.dueDate) || effectiveStatus` — falling back to the exact same `effectiveStatus` the row always used before, so an unlinked milestone (or one with no manual Due set yet) still reads its own plain status color rather than going tint-less. `computedL1MilestoneStatus()`/`effectiveStatus` themselves are otherwise untouched — they still drive the row's own status *badge* (`statusHtml`) exactly as before; only the row's own background reads from the new date-based function now. Reuses the exact same `--stat-*-bg` token throughout, so no other CSS changed.

**An L1 milestone's own name renders bold — a later, explicit user request ("make L1 milestones bold").** `.l1-milestone-name` (a new class wrapping just `esc(m.name)` inside `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s `nameHtml`, `font-weight: 700`) is scoped to the name text alone, not the whole cell — the linked-count/Reporting Level badges riding alongside it (`.l1-milestone-linked-count`/`.l1-milestone-reporting-level`) are already bold/colored in their own right and stay exactly as they were.

**The name is now inline-editable at Editor+ too — a later, explicit user request ("allow me to edit l1 milestons. i can only link and delete right now").** Due and Status were already directly editable on this row (`updateL1MilestoneDateField()`/`cycleL1MilestoneStatus()`), but the name itself — set once at creation via `saveL1MilestoneQuickAdd()` — had no way to be corrected afterward; Connect and Delete were the only two actions in the row's own Actions cell. Unlike an ordinary item's milestones, an L1 milestone has no parent modal to fall back on for this at all (an L1 Plan's own item modal is reachable only for renaming/deleting the *plan*, never for managing its milestones — see above), so the row itself is the only place this could ever live: `nameEditableHtml` swaps `.l1-milestone-name`'s plain `<span>` for a real, always-live `<input type="text">` at Editor+, the identical "no modal round-trip needed" treatment Due already gets, rather than a double-click-to-reveal affordance (which would be a genuinely different interaction model from the rest of this same row's own always-visible-editable fields). `updateL1MilestoneName(planId, milestoneId, value)` commits on blur, trims the incoming value, and no-ops (discards the edit, `render()` alone snaps the input back to the real stored name) when the trimmed result is blank or unchanged — the same "an edit that resolves to nothing doesn't get committed" stance the Action Log/Decision Log's own inline text edits (`updateActionLogText()`) already take.

`.l1-milestone-row input.l1-milestone-name` gets its own small CSS block rather than reusing `.flat-cell-input` outright (the class Action Log/Decision Log's own inline text edits share) — that class's `width: 100%` assumes the field owns its whole grid cell alone, which is true for an Action Item/Owner cell but not here: the name input shares its cell with the linked-count/Reporting Level badges riding after it. `field-sizing: content` (the same auto-grow mechanism `.minutes-action-item-row`'s own textarea already uses) sizes the input to its own text instead, so it sits inline before its sibling badges exactly like the `<span>` it replaces used to, no border/background at rest, an accent-ring on focus matching every other flat inline text edit in this app.

**An L1 Plan's own milestones are now drag-and-drop reorderable too — a later, explicit user request ("also allow rearraning of l1 milestons per drag and drop"), followed immediately by a second one ("i want cross-device sync") once the first cut turned out to be local-only.** `dragStartL1Milestone(e, milestoneId)`/`dropOnL1Milestone(e, planId, targetMilestoneId)` mirror `dragStartItem()`/`dropOnItem()`'s own grip-handle/whole-row-drop-target split (only the grip icon is `draggable="true"`, so dragging doesn't fight with selecting text in the name input right next to it; the whole `.l1-milestone-row` is the drop target). Unlike `dragStartCategoryMilestoneRow()`'s own template reorder (a plain array splice, safe there since `editingCategoryMilestones` is a modal working copy nothing else can see mid-edit), `plan.milestones` is live, synced data — a plain splice would move records around in *this device's own* array only, with nothing for a merge to ever pick up from another device, since array position was never something `mergeMilestonesArray()` looked at in the first place.

**The fix — the same one `reorderItem()`/`reorderWorkstream()` themselves needed for the identical reason (see "A user-reported gap: neither reorder actually reached another already-synced device" below) — is a real `m.order: Number` field on every L1 milestone, merged for free through the *existing*, unmodified per-milestone field merge (`mergeMilestonesArray()` already replaces a whole milestone object, `order` included, whenever an incoming copy's own `updatedAt` is newer — it just never had a reason to read `order` for anything before now).** `dropOnL1Milestone()` works on a `.slice().sort()`-by-`order` copy of `plan.milestones`, splices the dragged milestone to the target's position *within that copy*, then reassigns clean contiguous order values (0,1,2,…) back onto the real milestone objects — `plan.milestones`' own raw array position is never touched at all, and is no longer meaningful for display. Only a milestone whose `order` value actually changed gets a fresh `updatedAt` stamp, `reorderItem()`'s own "don't make an untouched record look freshly edited" discipline — which is what lets a later merge correctly recognize just the genuinely-moved milestones as newer, not the whole plan's worth. `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()` reads `sortedMilestones` (`plan.milestones.slice().sort((a,b) => (a.order||0)-(b.order||0))`) for display instead of raw array order — the one change that actually makes a merged-in `order` value visible at all.

`nextL1MilestoneOrder(plan)` — used by both `saveL1MilestoneQuickAdd()` and `importL1PlansFromExcel()` for a brand-new milestone's own starting `order` — is deliberately `Math.max(...existing orders) + 1`, not `plan.milestones.length`: a milestone removed from the middle leaves a gap (3 milestones at order 0/1/2, remove the middle one, the survivors keep 0/2 while `.length` drops to 2) that a length-based next-value would collide into. `removeL1Milestone()` itself never renumbers the survivors to close that gap — there's no need to, since sorting by `order` for display already works correctly regardless of gaps.

**Scoped to L1 milestones only — an ordinary item's own milestones have never had, and still don't have, a persisted `order` field.** `normalizeData()`'s per-milestone backfill (`it.itemType === 'l1plan' ? (missing → current array index) : delete m.order`) is the mirror image of `reportingLevel`'s own backfill one field up — each field only ever meaningful on one side of that same L1-vs-ordinary split. An ordinary item's milestones keep relying on plain array order the way "Category template edits sync to items already using that category" above already documents (a category-template reorder physically resorts the array itself, not a field) — untouched by any of this, and deliberately not extended to match, since that would be a materially larger change to a path this request never touched.

**The Actions cluster grew from a snug 56px (Connect + Delete only) to 120px to fit the new drag handle and Edit pencil alongside them — four real icons where there used to be two.** 120px matches this app's own established width for a real 4-icon actions cluster elsewhere (`.item-actions`, see "Column layout is a real CSS grid" above — 56px was only ever correctly sized for 3). This is a deliberate, accepted trade-off, not a regression nobody noticed: it breaks the "Status→right-edge distance matches `.l1-plans-list`" alignment invariant this exact row fought hard to establish earlier (documented at length in `.l1-milestone-header`/`.l1-milestone-row`'s own CSS comment) — the L1 Plan's own outer row never needs a drag handle or Edit action of its own (only its milestones do), so widening it to match would mean re-deriving its own carefully-tuned Milestones-badge spacer math too, for zero real benefit. Verified live via CDP that four real, differently-shaped interactive icons visibly overflowed the old 56px column before this width increase (the Delete icon rendered entirely past the row's own right edge), and that all four fit cleanly within the new 120px after it.

**The linked-milestones list itself (`l1LinkedHeaderHtml()`/`l1LinkedRowHtml()`) gained a sixth real column, Actual, between Due and Status — the "show" half of the original two-part request** ("show and consider actual date of attached scope items and milestones"). This is the *linked workstream milestone's own* real `actualDate` (plain read-only text, `—` when unset — identical treatment to how Due already rendered there), not the L1 milestone's own computed one — genuinely new information this list didn't show before, not a duplicate of anything else on the row. Adding a real column here — the one place in the whole L1 Plans alignment story (see "This feature's alignment story..." above for the earlier, painful history of that) where a **new** column was actually needed, not just a re-tuned width — necessarily shifts Status further right on `.l1-linked-row` than it sits on `.l1-milestone-row`/`.l1-plans-list` one and two levels up; there was no way to add six real columns' worth of width without moving something, so the "identical x-position across all three nesting levels" invariant those earlier passes fought hard to establish now only holds between those *other* two levels, not this one — an accepted, deliberate, documented trade-off, not a regression nobody noticed. (A later pass reserved a matching 110px column on `.l1-milestone-row` too, purely for Due alignment at first — see "This feature's alignment story..." below — which is exactly the slot the L1-level Actual column above now fills; the width was already correct before there was any real content for it.)

**Workstream/Scope Item widened over three later, explicit user follow-ups ("give the columns Workstream, Scope Item and Milestone more space", then "double the space of scope item", then "give scope item even more space. another 25%") — 110px/140px → 150px/180px → 150px/360px → 150px/450px, Milestone's own flexible minimum bumped 160px → 220px along the way.** Due/Actual/Status/the trailing blank 56px cell are all untouched throughout — widening only the first two fixed columns (Milestone stays the one flexible `minmax(...,1fr)` track) is what keeps the "identical x-position across nesting levels" invariant for Due/Status intact for the two levels it still applies to (see just above); `min-width` grew from 946px to 1356px to match (150+450+220+110+110+116+56 = 1212, +60 gaps +84 padding).

**The list is ordered by due date (or actual date, whichever's set) ascending — earliest first, latest at the bottom, undated last — an explicit user request, corrected once from its own first cut** ("order the linked scope items by due date date (or actual date) descending", then "latest should be on bottom"). `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()` sorts a local copy of `linked` (never `linkedWorkstreamMilestones()`'s own return order, which every other caller — `computedL1MilestoneStatus()`, `computedL1MilestoneActualDate()`, `l1PlanRollupRows()` — only ever reduces/counts over, order never mattering there) before mapping to rows: `actualDate || dueDate || '9999-99-99'`, compared via `localeCompare` ascending. Actual takes precedence over Due when both are set — the same "prefer the already-happened date over the merely-planned one" convention Dashboard's own Overdue/Upcoming feed already established (see `toEntry()` under "Dashboard mode" below) — and the `'9999-99-99'` high sentinel pushes anything with neither date to the bottom, the exact same missing-date fallback `sortedActionLog()`'s own ascending soonest-due-first sort already uses.

**Attached (linked) milestones get their own, separate delayed-color check — the "consider" half of the two-part request, confirmed directly as a distinct comparison basis from everything above** ("colorcode if due date and actual date [of attached milestones] are past L1 milestone due date"). `l1LinkedRowHtml(item, milestone, l1DueDate)` gained a third parameter: the *parent L1 milestone's own* `m.dueDate`, passed through from `l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s own `m.dueDate` when building `linkedRowsHtml` — the same manual/imported value described above, not a rollup of any kind (there's no rolled-up Due to speak of any more). This reuse is what makes the whole design cohere into one consistent story after Due's own rollup was reversed: `m.dueDate` is now the *single* source of truth for "this L1 milestone's due date," used identically for display, for its own Actual's delayed-color check just above, and for this one — a linked milestone's own `dueDate` or `actualDate` later than that same value renders in `var(--stat-red)` with a "Past the L1 milestone's own due date" tooltip; `null` (never manually set) means nothing to compare against, so nothing is ever flagged.

**`computedL1MilestoneStatus()`/`computeL1PlanStatus()`/`computedL1MilestoneActualDate()` are all computed live at read time, never stored back onto anything** — Due is the one exception among this feature's rollups, deliberately: it's `m.dueDate` itself, a real stored field, not a computed function at all (see above for why). A linked workstream milestone's own status/actualDate can change from any number of different code paths on the Planning board (the inline click-to-cycle badge, the item modal, a category-template sync); hooking every one of them just to keep a cached value in sync would be exactly the fragile, easy-to-miss plumbing this pattern avoids. Because nothing is cached, toggling a link (`toggleL1MilestoneLink()`) needs no special recompute-and-stamp step of its own beyond its existing `save(); render();` — the very next render already picks up the correct values at every level, and so does any later, completely unrelated render triggered by editing a linked workstream milestone elsewhere.

**`itemRowHtml()`'s `displayStatus`** gained an `isL1Plan` branch reading `computeL1PlanStatus(it.id) || 'not-started'` — an L1 Plan's own status badge on its row now always reflects the live rollup, never the plan's own stored `status` field. **`l1MilestoneRowsHtml()`'s own status cell** switches between the computed (read-only `<span>`, tooltip naming how many milestones it's computed from) and manual (clickable `<button>`, click-to-cycle) rendering based on whether `computedL1MilestoneStatus(m.id)` is non-null — exactly mirroring the computed-vs-manual toggle every other "has real data to compute from, or doesn't" field in this app already uses. **`cycleL1MilestoneStatus()` refuses outright once a milestone has any link** (`if (computedL1MilestoneStatus(milestoneId)) return;`), defense-in-depth behind the row itself never rendering a clickable button for one.

**The plan's own stored `plan.status` field (still stamped by `cycleL1MilestoneStatus()`/`removeL1Milestone()`/`saveL1MilestoneQuickAdd()` via `computedStatusFromMilestones(plan.milestones)`) is now vestigial for display purposes, not wrong to keep updating.** It reads each L2 milestone's raw stored `status` directly, which is correct for an *unlinked* milestone but can't reflect a *linked* one's own computed value — so it's no longer what the row actually displays (`computeL1PlanStatus()` is). Left in place anyway, since removing the stamping calls would save nothing observable and risk missing some other, not-yet-audited reader of `it.status`.

**A read-only chain-link indicator surfaces the link from the workstream side — without it, a link would be entirely invisible from Planning unless someone specifically opened the L1 Plan's own connect modal.** `l1LinkIndicatorHtml(m)` returns a small `fa-link` icon whenever `m.l1MilestoneIds` resolves at least one real link (mapping every id through `findL1Milestone()`, filtering out anything stale — the same silent-degrade-to-nothing treatment an orphaned reference gets elsewhere in this app), or an empty string when none do. **One icon regardless of how many links resolve** — the tooltip lists every one, one `<plan> — <milestone>` line per link, rather than rendering a separate icon per link (which would be a much bigger visual change for what's still meant to be a subtle, glanceable marker). `milestoneRowsHtml()` appends it inline, immediately after the milestone's own name text — deliberately placed *inside* the existing, already-flexible name cell rather than claiming a new grid column, so `--item-grid-cols` (already documented above as extremely tightly tuned with zero spare columns) needed zero changes for this feature. Purely informational — not clickable; the connect modal on the L1 Plan side, or the picker modal on the workstream side (see "The Unlinked tab and L1 milestone picker" below), are still the only ways to change a link.

**The inner milestone list gets a bespoke renderer and its own fixed CSS grid (`.l1-milestone-header`/`.l1-milestone-row`), not `milestoneRowsHtml()`/`--item-grid-cols`.** Forcing a "linked count" column and a "+ Add milestone" affordance into the shared grid risked a real regression to Planning's own tuned layout for no benefit, since an L1 Plan renders on a completely separate page with no need to visually align with Planning's own rows — the identical reasoning Action Log/Decision Log/Dashboard's Dependency tracker already used to justify their own bespoke grids rather than forcing themselves into the shared item grid. `l1MilestoneHeaderHtml()`/`l1MilestoneRowsHtml(plan)` lay out a leading Chevron (see below) / Milestone / Due / Status / Actions, five columns, `grid-template-columns: 20px minmax(160px,1fr) 110px 100px 56px`. **The Connect action is a plain icon-only `.row-icon-btn` (`fa-link`, `.l1-linked` tinting it accent-colored once anything is linked) — a later, explicit user request** ("replace the big linking item, with a simple like item (like the delete icon)"), reversing this column's original shape (a wider pill button reading "N linked"/"Connect"). **The "how many are linked" count itself moved to a small badge right next to the milestone's own name — the same request's second half** ("move the informtion of how many milestones are linke next to the l1 milestone name"): `nameHtml` appends `<span class="l1-milestone-linked-count">N linked</span>` immediately after the name whenever `linkedCount` is non-zero, omitted entirely (not a "0 linked" badge) when nothing is.

**Connect and Delete share one trailing Actions column, rendered together as a small `.l1-milestone-actions` flex cluster (`display:flex; gap:2px`).** `.l1-milestone-actions` deliberately isn't `itemRowHtml()`'s own `.item-actions` reused directly — that class's base rule is `visibility: hidden`, only flipped visible by a `.item-row:hover` selector that doesn't apply to `.l1-milestone-row` at all, and these two icons have always rendered plainly, not hover-gated; a same-layout, separately-named class avoids silently changing that. The whole cluster still renders as a bare, empty `<span></span>` below Editor, same as any other action cell in this app.
